a:T407,The derivation starts from the GRPO update with group-relative advantages, KL anchoring to a reference policy, momentum, and periodic refresh of the sampling policy. Under a mean-field assumption, the high-dimensional policy state is summarized by the expected reward p(theta)=E[R], whose evolution is governed by a one-dimensional drift g(p) with a stable fixed point. A second-order backward-error expansion of the discrete update then yields a stochastically forced damped oscillator. The effective mass, damping, and stiffness are given in closed form from optimizer hyperparameters plus one measured curvature scale. Momentum supplies inertia. Off-policy lag from delayed refresh adds inertia while reducing damping. Group size enters as a noise temperature rather than as a determinant of the mean trajectory. The familiar single-exponential reward saturation law appears as the overdamped limit of this oscillator. Keeping the inertial term explains an early slow-start inflection that a single exponential cannot represent.b:T53c,LOTUS inserts K learnable padded latent blocks, each with c latent tokens, between the question and the answer. The whole sequence is processed R times by the same Transformer backbone, reusing weights across iterations. This recurrent-depth design converts a length-N explicit CoT into R rounds of parallel dense computation, with R much smaller than N.

The central training signal is parallel cross-entropy supervision on the post-loop latents. Each latent position is read through the base model’s own LM head and trained to predict the corresponding gold CoT token. This gives the latent trace a direct token-level target. The design differs from autoregressive latent methods such as Coconut, CODI, and SIM-CoT because it does not generate latent thoughts one by one. It also differs from methods such as PCCoT and KaVa, which reduce sequential bottlenecks but rely on more indirect grounding signals such as teacher hidden states or compressed KV-cache targets.

The paper also introduces LOTUS-aux, where the same supervision is routed through an auxiliary decoder conditioned on all latents and trained with teacher forcing. The auxiliary decoder is used only during training. It can match the direct route at the 3B scale, but it degrades on smaller backbones, which makes the direct LM-head route the more robust design choice.c:T443,The main model is Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct. On GSM8K, LOTUS comes within 1 point of explicit CoT. On the out-of-domain math average, it surpasses explicit CoT while reducing thought-phase latency by 2.5x in the math-expression setting. On a more verbose natural-language CoT stress test, it matches explicit CoT accuracy and reduces thought-phase latency by 6.9x.

The ablations support two key claims. First, direct parallel supervision is important; without CoT-token grounding, the latent trace is less reliable as a reasoning carrier. Second, the looped architecture needs enough block width and loop depth. The benefit is not obtained simply by inserting latent tokens into a standard forward pass.

The interpretability analysis is also notable. Reading post-loop latents through the base LM head recovers most gold intermediate tokens, and sometimes assigns probability mass to alternative valid reasoning steps that were not used as training targets. This is evidence that the latent states are aligned with language-level reasoning rather than only optimized for final-answer accuracy.d:T6ab,The method, BlockPilot, is a lightweight block-size policy predictor invoked once after prefilling. The authors first run systematic block-size sweeps and show two empirical facts: the sample-wise optimal block size is not constant, and for most samples it lies near the training block size rather than anywhere in a large global action space. This motivates a local discrete classification problem instead of online search or a heavier RL-style controller.

For state representation, BlockPilot does not consume the full hidden-state sequence or add a complex sequence encoder. Instead, it uses the target model’s predictive distribution for the final token after prefilling. The paper argues that this distribution already aggregates full-context information through autoregressive attention, so it captures both local uncertainty and the stability of the upcoming continuation. The action space is then defined as a small neighborhood around the training block size, which keeps labeling and deployment simple.

A notable design choice is how conservative the integration is. The method does not modify the target model, the diffusion draft model, or the verification procedure. It only decides which block size to use before decoding starts. In other words, the gain comes from policy selection on top of an existing speculative decoding stack, not from improving draft quality itself.

The paper also leans heavily on the locality observation. If optimal block sizes were globally scattered, policy learning would be noisy and brittle. Because they cluster around the training configuration, the problem becomes closer to calibration and routing, which explains why a small predictor can work at all.e:T489,The experiments have three main parts. First, the paper validates the phenomenon through block-size sweeps on multiple datasets, showing a persistent gap between a fixed default policy and sample-wise optimal choices. Second, it analyzes the distribution of optimal block sizes and finds a clear local concentration around the training configuration. Third, it reports end-to-end gains on top of existing diffusion-based speculative decoding. The headline number given in the paper is an acceptance length of 5.92 and a 4.20× speedup on Qwen3-4B at temperature 1.

The most important things to inspect are not just the headline speedup, but the supporting details: how large the variance of optimal block size is across tasks, whether the predictor overhead is negligible relative to the gain, how robust the policy is under different temperatures, and how sensitive performance is to the width of the local action space. If the full paper includes ablations on predictor inputs, label construction, and failure cases, those are more informative than the main table because they determine whether the approach can transfer to other speculative decoding setups.f:T95a,RaBitQCache compresses high-dimensional key vectors into rotated binary codes, then uses very cheap binary-low-bit arithmetic to estimate query-key similarity during decoding. The method has several layers.

First is representation compression. During prefill, each key vector is randomly rotated and then mapped to the vertices of a unit hypercube, producing a binary code. The random rotation is important: it preserves geometric structure better than naive sign binarization and reduces coordinate-aligned bias. This design is clearly inspired by JL-style dimensionality reduction and approximate nearest-neighbor methods.

Second is the proxy-score construction. During decoding, the method does not compute full-precision inner products between the query and all keys. Instead, it quantizes the query to INT4 and computes a lightweight interaction with the binary keys. The resulting score is used as an estimator of the true attention score. The paper emphasizes two properties: the estimator is unbiased, and it comes with a rigorous error bound. Unbiasedness matters because it avoids systematic distortion of token importance. The error bound matters because it enables not just rough ranking but magnitude-aware estimation, which is exactly what adaptive Top-p retrieval needs.

Third is the retrieval policy. Unlike fixed Top-k methods, RaBitQCache uses the estimated attention mass to perform adaptive Top-p retrieval. This is a meaningful design choice because attention sparsity varies widely across layers, heads, and tasks. A fixed k is often either too conservative, wasting compute, or too aggressive, hurting accuracy. Top-p is a better match to heterogeneous attention distributions, but only if the proxy can estimate score magnitudes reasonably well rather than merely preserving order.

Fourth is the systems layer. The paper implements hardware-aware kernels and scheduling, including asynchronous pipelining and lazy updates, to hide the overhead of proxy computation and data movement. This part is important because many sparse or offloading methods save arithmetic on paper but lose the benefit in practice due to gather/scatter overhead and transfer latency.

Relative to methods such as Quest or DS, the differences are clear: finer retrieval granularity, a proxy closer to the true inner product, and support for adaptive Top-p rather than fixed-budget selection.10:T5c2,From the provided full-text excerpt, the paper evaluates long-context inference acceleration and claims clear gains over prior sparse-attention and KV-offloading baselines. The excerpt does not include the full result tables, but the experimental story is already visible. First, RaBitQCache improves long-context inference efficiency without requiring access to the full KV cache and while keeping model quality largely intact. Second, its advantage comes from more accurate token-importance estimation, which means lower quality loss at the same retrieval budget, or lower budget at similar quality. Third, the systems optimizations appear necessary to convert algorithmic savings into end-to-end latency gains.

When reading the full paper, four experimental blocks deserve attention. One is the correlation or error analysis between the proxy score and the true attention score; that is the foundation of the method. Another is the comparison between Top-k and Top-p retrieval, especially across layers, heads, and tasks with different sparsity patterns. A third is the end-to-end comparison against baselines such as Quest, DS, and static-position retrieval in terms of latency, throughput, and memory or bandwidth usage. A fourth is the ablation over random rotation, binary code length, INT4 quantization, lazy updates, and asynchronous scheduling. The most convincing evidence will likely be an improved accuracy-latency frontier rather than a single speedup number.11:T4a3,A 25-35 minute skim should be enough to judge the paper. Start with the introduction's diagnosis of prior dynamic KV retrieval: inaccurate proxies and no error bounds. That framing explains why the paper insists on a theoretically grounded estimator. Then go straight to the method section and read the definition of the proxy score together with the main theorem. Check carefully whether the guarantee applies to the raw query-key inner product, the attention logits, or something after normalization. After that, inspect the systems diagram to see whether asynchronous pipelining and lazy updates are core to the design or just implementation details. Finally, read the main results and ablations with an eye on the accuracy-latency tradeoff, not just isolated speedup numbers.

If time is limited, prioritize three parts: the main theoretical proposition, the Top-p retrieval experiments, and the end-to-end system table. For deeper follow-up, two questions are worth asking: how stable the rotated binary representation is under layer-wise distribution shift, and whether the same proxy machinery could be reused for training-time memory compression or retrieval-augmented attention.12:T6fe,The method, Fork-Think with Confidence, has three stages. First, instead of sampling many traces immediately, it generates a single greedy seed path. Second, it scans this seed path to identify promising forking points. The intuition comes from pivot tokens: some token positions have disproportionate influence on downstream generation, so branching near them can produce meaningfully different reasoning trajectories. The paper uses model confidence to detect such positions. Low-confidence points are treated as likely decision bottlenecks where alternative continuations may be valuable. Third, the method samples multiple continuations only from those selected points and aggregates them into the final answer.

The main difference from prior work is not a stronger verifier or a more complex scorer. It is a different placement of sampling budget. Standard parallel thinking branches right after the prompt, which is simple but often wasteful: early randomness creates many superficially diverse but low-value traces. Fork-Think keeps the prefix deterministic, then injects diversity later at positions that are more likely to affect the final reasoning outcome. The paper also emphasizes compatibility with existing pruning and aggregation methods, so this should be read as a front-end compute allocation strategy rather than a full replacement for downstream selection.

Mechanistically, the method relies on two assumptions. First, the greedy seed path must reach a reasonably representative intermediate state. Second, token-level confidence must correlate with branching value. The experiments support both to some extent, but they also expose the main limitation: if the seed path is already badly off-track, later branching may only explore variations around a wrong backbone.13:T604,The paper evaluates on three models and three reasoning benchmarks. The headline result reported in the abstract and main text is that, compared with standard parallel thinking, Fork-Think reduces total token cost by up to about 30% and end-to-end runtime by up to about 57%, while maintaining or improving task performance. The runtime gain is plausible because branching happens later, so more prefix computation is shared and fewer low-value continuations are generated.

The ablations are the most informative part. One key study varies the forking position and shows that later branching points often outperform branching at the beginning. This is an important result: the benefit of parallel reasoning does not come from maximizing diversity as early as possible, but from placing diversity where the reasoning trajectory is genuinely unstable. Another set of ablations compares different confidence-based selection rules for identifying fork points. A third set studies compatibility with different aggregation schemes, supporting the claim that Fork-Think is not tied to one specific voting or reranking mechanism.

For a careful reader, the most useful signal is not the absolute benchmark score but the compute-performance tradeoff curve. The method appears to shift budget toward higher-value branches under the same or lower inference cost. If the full paper includes results over different numbers of forks, temperatures, or seed decoding choices, those tables are likely more revealing than the main leaderboard-style summary.14:T400,A 20-30 minute skim is enough to extract the main idea. Start with Figure 1, because the conceptual shift from think-first-then-decide to decide-first-then-think is the center of the paper. Then read the method section carefully enough to pin down how confidence is computed: token probability, entropy, margin, or some normalized uncertainty score. After that, inspect the main results table and check whether accuracy, token cost, and wall-clock time are all reported together. Finally, read the ablation on fork position. That section carries much of the paper's actual evidence.

If deciding whether to follow up, focus on three items: the method overview figure, the fork-position ablation, and the efficiency comparison against standard parallel thinking. If reading more deeply, two questions are worth keeping in mind: whether confidence is only a cheap proxy for branch value, and whether the same decide-first-then-think principle can be extended to tree search, speculative reasoning, or verifier-guided decoding.15:T411,The work separates success into (i) Lean validity (compilation/type correctness) and (ii) semantic faithfulness to the original natural-language theorem. Since Lean cannot decide semantic equivalence, they introduce a calibrated semantic filter: multiple independent LLM judges score whether the generated Lean declaration preserves meaning; results are reported under a strict consensus rule, and a human expert audit calibrates how conservative/precise the consensus labels are.

Beyond measurement, they run a factorial ablation over three intervention axes:
- Parametric drafting: the base model producing an initial Lean statement.
- Mathlib/context grounding: retrieving/providing relevant definitions and library context.
- Lean elaboration feedback: iterative repair using elaborator/compiler feedback.

The key design choice is to quantify which interventions primarily improve validity, which improve faithfulness, and which mainly reduce cost/iterations—rather than treating “verifier feedback helps” as a monolithic effect.16:T808,Vega is built around a shared vocabulary and a hybrid AR-diffusion design. Text and visual inputs are mapped into a unified discrete token space, but the model does not autoregress over dense pixel tokens. Instead, the AR component predicts semantic visual representations at the keyframe level, modeling high-level scene dynamics and state transitions. A diffusion decoder then acts as a renderer, turning those semantic embeddings into continuous, high-fidelity video. This division of labor is the core design choice: AR is used where discrete semantic transitions matter, while diffusion is used where local detail, realism, and temporal smoothness matter. The paper argues that this is better suited to video than pure AR, which suffers from excessive token counts and poor visual quality, and better integrated than prior hybrid systems that rely on frozen understanding backbones. On the generation side, Vega adopts a video-centric formulation in which multiple tasks are cast as video generation; image editing is treated as a special case of a two-frame video. To compensate for the detail loss of semantic tokens, the model uses noise-controlled visual conditioning, which preserves fine-grained appearance information and improves temporal consistency during rendering. On the understanding side, the paper introduces a dual-flow token selection mechanism inspired by the slowfast idea. A detail flow uses stronger spatial compression to capture rapidly changing local content, while a pivot flow uses lower compression to retain slower, structurally important temporal anchors. This is not just token dropping; it is a hierarchical temporal allocation strategy meant to preserve useful dynamics under a tight token budget. Another notable component is the generative visual loss used as auxiliary supervision for understanding. That choice suggests the model is trained to maintain a representation that is not only discriminative but also generatively reconstructible, which is a meaningful design direction for native multimodal pretraining.17:T643,From the available full-text content, the experimental story appears to focus on three claims. First, the unified framework can support both video generation and video understanding without reducing one side to a weak add-on. The paper states that Vega performs competitively across multiple generation and understanding tasks, and highlights that image editing fits naturally into the same video-centric formulation. Second, semantic-space autoregression is argued to be more suitable for video than pixel-token autoregression, mainly because it reduces token count, mitigates temporal redundancy, and leaves high-fidelity rendering to the diffusion decoder. Third, the paper claims that generative supervision improves understanding: the auxiliary generative visual loss provides additional learning signal for video understanding, implying an ablation where removing that loss hurts understanding performance. A second important ablation likely concerns the dual-flow selection mechanism, comparing it against simpler token compression or single-flow baselines to show that pivot/detail separation better preserves multi-timescale temporal information. The provided text does not include the full result tables, so it is not possible to reliably report exact model sizes, dataset scales, or numerical gains. For reading purposes, the most informative results are likely the ablations: semantic tokens versus pixel tokens, AR-only versus hybrid AR-diffusion, with and without noise-controlled conditioning, with and without generative visual loss, and dual-flow versus simpler token selection schemes.18:T45d,This is best read as a unified-modeling paper rather than a pure video-generation paper. Start with the introduction and method overview to pin down what is actually unified: not a single monolithic AR decoder, but a shared semantic token space with AR for latent dynamics and diffusion for rendering. Then focus on the sections describing the shared vocabulary, semantic vision tokens, dual-flow selection, and noise-controlled visual conditioning. Two questions are worth keeping in mind while reading: why does video need a different unification strategy from images, and why is the chosen split ‘semantic AR plus pixel diffusion’ preferable to pure AR or pure diffusion? In the experiments, prioritize ablations over headline benchmark tables. The most useful evidence is whether generative loss really helps understanding, whether dual-flow beats simpler token compression, and whether the video-centric formulation gives a clean path to tasks such as image editing. Around 30 minutes should be enough for a first-pass assessment; a deeper read should center on the architecture diagrams and ablation tables.19:T504,ADAPT is built on an observed two-stage shift: an early visually grounded stage where cross-attention focuses on text-relevant evidence, followed by a prior-dominated stage where cross-attention degrades. They characterize degradation with three signals: attention drift (unfocused or spatially biased), reduced responsiveness to newly generated tokens (lower attention change rate over time), and reduced overall attention mass on visual tokens across layers/positions.

The method has three coupled components:
- Visual Enhance: extract early-stage multi-layer cross-attention and refine it into a semantics-aware anchor (including positional-bias correction).
- Attention-Supervised Inference: monitor later-stage cross-attention against the anchor and apply sparse corrective steering when degradation patterns appear.
- Visual Attention Guidance DPO: preference-tune using pairs constructed under different visual evidence conditions (anchor-enhanced images as chosen vs noise images as rejected) to encourage visually grounded behavior.

Compared to output-only mitigation (logit penalties, standard DPO/RLHF), ADAPT treats cross-attention trajectories as first-class objects to supervise and control, combining inference-time correction with training-time preference alignment.1a:T546,BEST-RQ-2 makes two core changes to the original BEST-RQ design. First, it replaces the Conformer encoder with a Vision Transformer operating on patchified log-mel spectrograms. Unlike convolutional Conformer layers that rely on dense, contiguous input layout, ViT uses position-aware self-attention that natively supports arbitrary subsets of input patches, making encoder-predictor decomposition feasible. Second, drawing design inspiration from Audio-JEPA, masked prediction is split into two separate stages: a context ViT encoder processes only unmasked patches to produce contextualized representations, and a lightweight ViT predictor combines these context representations with position-aware mask embeddings to predict discrete codes for masked positions. The predictor is only used during pretraining and discarded entirely at inference, so downstream compute and model size remain identical to the original BEST-RQ. The core design choice from BEST-RQ is retained: discrete targets are generated via a frozen random projection quantizer with no learned codebook, enabling stable cross-entropy training. A controlled baseline, BEST-RQ (ViT), uses the same ViT encoder but keeps the original single-stage in-place masked prediction pipeline, to isolate performance contributions from the architecture swap versus the two-stage decomposition.1b:T514,The analysis first disentangles two correlated but distinct signals: position-level predictive entropy measures the model's total uncertainty at a sequence position, while sampled token probability measures the likelihood of the specific token that was generated at that position. High-entropy positions can produce both high- and low-probability tokens; low-probability tokens can appear either at high-entropy positions (where the model is uncertain, providing useful learning signal) or low-entropy positions (where the model is highly confident but sampled an outlier, producing noisy, unstable gradients). The Relative Surprisal Index is defined to combine both signals, measuring the relative unexpectedness of the sampled token against the model's full predictive distribution at the position. Under mild assumptions, RSI has a direct analytical connection to the magnitude of token-level policy gradients, making it a direct proxy for token importance. The RSI-S selection scheme filters two categories of unproductive tokens before gradient updates: low-surprisal tokens at low-entropy positions (already mastered, near-zero gradient) and high-surprisal tokens at low-entropy positions (outlier samples, unstable gradients), retaining only tokens that provide stable, useful learning signal.1c:T899,The central idea is competing rewards over different segments of the same trajectory. Instead of assigning a single scalar reward to the full rollout, the method splits generation into a reasoning segment and a final answer segment, and scores them separately. The reasoning segment is rewarded for exploring increasingly harmful or unsafe interpretations. The answer segment is rewarded for producing a safe final response conditioned on that preceding unsafe exploration. The intended behavior is not globally “safe-looking” reasoning, but controlled exploration followed by safe recovery.

Several design choices matter. First, the paper argues that optimization mismatch between the competing objectives leads to unstable training. If the exploration reward and the safe-answer reward are not balanced in how they are sampled or optimized, the model can collapse to one side: either unsafe exploration without recovery, or conservative refusal without meaningful exploration. Second, the authors show that dense outcome-level rewards are still too coarse for this setting. They do not provide enough credit assignment to tell the model which part of the trajectory should be exploratory and which part should be controlled. This motivates process-based rewards at the segment level on sampled rollouts. Third, the method keeps exploration and recovery within one continuous chain of thought, rather than outsourcing safety to a separate classifier or a detached control module. That is an important distinction: the goal is to train safety as a reasoning dynamic, not as a front-end filter.

Relative to prior safety-RL methods such as TARS, the difference is not merely a modified reward function. The underlying training assumption changes. Earlier work largely treats reasoning as a tool for deciding whether to refuse. This paper treats reasoning as a space where the model must be allowed to inspect unsafe possibilities under control; otherwise it cannot resolve ambiguous prompts well. The robustness analysis around pre-filling attacks reinforces this point: if a model has never learned to recover from unsafe intermediate reasoning, steering its chain of thought becomes much easier.1d:T62d,The main model is SEAR-1.5B. The headline result is about the safety–refusal trade-off rather than a single benchmark score. The paper reports that SEAR achieves the best trade-off among open models in its size class, outperforming the closest baseline, TARS. It also claims that despite having only 1.5B parameters, SEAR performs comparably to the much larger TARS-7B on this trade-off, which suggests that the gain is not a minor tuning effect but a meaningful change in how the model uses reasoning.

The most important experiments fall into four groups. First, the paper analyzes existing safety-trained reasoning models and shows that their CoT often behaves like a rubber stamp rather than a mechanism for ambiguity resolution. That diagnostic section is important because it motivates the method. Second, there are reward-design ablations comparing single outcome rewards, dense but non-process rewards, and true segment-level process rewards. The conclusion is that only the last option reliably learns the desired combination of unsafe exploration and safe answering. Third, the paper studies optimization stability and shows that mismatched optimization across competing rewards leads to unstable behavior. Fourth, it evaluates robustness to adversarial attacks, especially attacks that target the reasoning process such as pre-filling. SEAR is reported to be more robust under such attacks. The provided text clearly gives the model size and the comparison to TARS-7B; exact benchmark numbers and attack-rate deltas need to be read from the result tables and appendix.1e:T430,All runs use rsLoRA with rank 32, alpha 64, no dropout, and adapters on all attention and feed-forward projection matrices. Training uses assistant-only loss, cosine learning-rate schedule, and 50 warmup steps. The misalignment-triggering datasets cover insecure code, bad medical advice, risky financial advice, and extreme sports advice. Evaluation follows the Betley et al. setup: 142 alignment questions, GPT-4o scoring for coherence and alignment, and exclusion of responses with coherence below 50.

The study has three technical pieces. First, it runs controlled sweeps over optimizers, datasets, batch sizes, model sizes, and model families. Second, it models the relationship between final loss and alignment, checking whether optimizer effects are merely mediated by lower loss. Third, it analyzes the singular values of the learned LoRA adapters. The spectral analysis motivates a direct intervention: adding a loss term that encourages a flatter singular-value spectrum, then measuring whether alignment can be recovered without paying much training-loss cost.1f:T45f,The initial sweep covers 4 smaller Qwen3 models, 4 optimizers, 4 datasets, and 2 batch sizes, for 128 one-epoch runs at learning rate 1e-5. The scale sweep uses Adam across 12 models from three families: Gemma 3 at 270M/1B/4B/12B, Llama at 1B/3B/8B, and Qwen3 at 4B/8B/30B-A3B/32B/235B-A22B. This adds 96 runs across the four datasets and two batch sizes.

The key quantitative findings are clear. Optimizers produce roughly a 7x spread in misalignment rate, consistently across Qwen3 sizes. With Adam fixed, model scale and family have negligible independent effects. On Qwen3-8B, log training loss explains 74% of alignment variance; adding optimizer stratification brings adjusted R2 to 0.94, while dataset, learning rate, and batch size add little. Checkpoint analysis shows that optimizers follow distinct paths in loss-alignment space, and after enough training, optimizer identity can matter more than final loss. A flatter-spectrum regularizer substantially improves alignment for Adam and Lion with little loss penalty, but hurts SGD, which suggests that the spectral mechanism is real but optimizer-dependent.20:T84d,作者提出了一个 surrogate fidelity 的层级框架，按从弱到强、从观测到干预再到机制的顺序，定义四类一致性：prediction fidelity、attribution fidelity、representation fidelity、cross-level fidelity。这里最巧的一点，是他们把 binary classification 场景下的 log-odds 作为 API-compatible 的表示读出量。因为对正负类 token 的 log-prob 做差时，softmax 的归一化常数会抵消，所以 log-odds 可以写成 residual representation 在一个 readout direction 上的线性投影。这样一来，即便拿不到完整 logits 或内部激活，只要 API 提供 top-K logprobs，仍然可以构造与表示几何直接相关的标量。

在此基础上，prediction fidelity 衡量 surrogate 与 target 在输出层面的预测一致性；attribution fidelity 则看输入扰动后，两个模型的输出变化是否一致，作者主要用 leave-one-out ablations 来做因果归因；representation fidelity 需要白盒访问，比较模型内部表示对同样干预的响应；cross-level fidelity 则更进一步，问 surrogate 的内部表示能否预测 target 的 attribution。这个层级设计有两个优点。其一，它遵循“最小权限原则”：要验证什么层级的主张，就只要求与之匹配的访问权限，而不是默认必须白盒。其二，它把很多过去混在一起的“模型相似性”拆开了：输出像，不等于归因像；归因像，也不等于内部表示真的同构。

全文还有一个值得注意的分析点：作者讨论了 access–validity inversion。某些高访问权限下很容易得到的白盒信号，比如 attention pattern 相似性、扰动幅度等，看起来信息很多，但未必能预测更有因果意义的 black-box attribution agreement。也就是说，能看到更多内部量，不代表你测到的东西更接近真正可迁移的解释。这个判断对 mechanistic interpretability 社区是有针对性的，因为很多常用分析工具默认“白盒信号更可信”，而作者的结果表明至少在跨模型迁移上不能这么简单看。21:T7c7,The paper proposes a hierarchy of surrogate fidelity metrics: prediction fidelity, attribution fidelity, representation fidelity, and cross-level fidelity. The setup is instantiated for binary classification tasks. A key technical observation is that binary log-odds can be recovered from API-accessible log-probabilities and are exactly linear in the model representation. Because the softmax normalization constant cancels when taking the difference between the positive and negative class log-probabilities, the resulting log-odds correspond to a projection of the residual representation onto a readout direction. This gives the framework a clean geometric interpretation while remaining compatible with restricted APIs.

On top of that, prediction fidelity measures agreement at the output level. Attribution fidelity measures whether the two models respond similarly to causal input interventions, implemented through leave-one-out ablations. Representation fidelity requires white-box access and compares how internal representations respond to the same interventions. Cross-level fidelity asks whether the surrogate’s internal representations can predict the target’s attributions. The hierarchy is well designed for two reasons. First, it follows a least-privilege principle: each claim should require only the level of access needed to test it. Second, it separates forms of similarity that are often conflated. Similar predictions do not imply similar attributions, and similar attributions do not automatically imply shared internal mechanisms.

A notable conceptual point is the access–validity inversion discussed in the paper. Some high-access white-box signals, such as attention similarity or perturbation magnitude, may look rich but fail to predict the more causally relevant black-box attribution agreement. That is a pointed critique of a common assumption in interpretability work: more internal access does not automatically mean a more transferable explanation.22:T45d,The evaluation spans 11 models from four families—Llama, Qwen, GPT, and Gemini—covering scales from 0.5B to 70B, and uses three binary classification benchmarks. This design matters because it includes both open and closed models, within-family scaling comparisons, and cross-family comparisons.

The central empirical result is a dissociation between prediction fidelity and attribution fidelity. Prediction agreement is often much higher than attribution agreement, meaning that models can align on the label while differing sharply in which parts of the input causally drive the decision. The paper also reports cases where models appear close in representation-level or output-level terms but still diverge substantially in attribution. Another important finding is the access–validity inversion: common white-box signals do not reliably predict the more causally meaningful attribution fidelity. The provided text does not include all numeric tables, but it clearly establishes the model coverage, the benchmark type, and the main conclusion that output agreement is a poor proxy for explanation agreement.23:T410,For a skim, start with the introduction and preliminaries. The introduction frames the problem cleanly: the issue is not whether a given interpretability tool is useful in isolation, but whether open-model findings can be transferred to closed models. In the preliminaries, pay attention to the derivation of log-odds as a linear readout from representation space. That is the technical hinge that makes the API-compatible part of the framework work.

Then read the section defining the fidelity hierarchy and the main experimental results. Focus on two questions: how large the gap is between prediction and attribution fidelity, and whether within-family surrogates are materially more reliable than cross-family ones. If you only have 25 to 35 minutes, the framework definition plus the main result figures are enough. If you want to use this paper to design audits or interpretability studies, also read the implementation details for leave-one-out attribution and the appendix discussion of why some white-box similarity measures fail.24:T698,The paper has three main technical components. First, it characterizes the native discrete gauge of transformer residual streams under coordinate-preserving parameterizations. For LayerNorm, the residual chart has permutation symmetry up to a global sign. For RMSNorm with generic per-channel gain, the correct group is the hyperoctahedral group B_d = S_d ⋉ {±1}^d, meaning arbitrary coordinate permutations plus independent sign flips. The paper also clarifies an important distinction: broader orthogonal freedom can be recovered only after absorbing the RMSNorm gain into adjacent weights, but that changes the coordinate system and no longer preserves sparse coordinate identity. Second, it proposes sign-marginalized activation matching. Instead of matching coordinates by signed correlation, it matches by absolute correlation |<h_i, h'_j>| and recovers the sign afterward from the matched pair. This is the right objective because the true counterpart of a coordinate may be maximally anti-correlated under a negative gauge sign. The authors prove a sharp population-level failure mode for signed-correlation matching in a decorrelated setting: if half of the true signs are negative, the correct entries become the most negative ones in their rows, so maximizing signed correlation systematically avoids them even with unlimited probes. Third, it introduces transport via a checkpoint atlas. Rather than matching only two endpoints, the method composes local B_d gauges saved along a training trajectory. This exploits the fact that nearby checkpoints differ only slightly, so local matches have higher margins and can be chained into a much more reliable global transport map.25:T62e,The experiments are designed to show how omission of signs fails in practice. On gauge-recorded LLM activations, signed-cost recovery stays near 50%, while sign-marginalized recovery reaches 100%, matching the theoretical obstruction. On a gauge-instrumented Qwen2.5-1.5B finetuning trajectory of 1500 steps, with a random B_d gauge applied at step 0 and the induced correspondence recorded as ground truth, direct endpoint matching recovers 60.3% of coordinates. Transport-via-base raises this to 91.1%, whereas endpoint-via-base without local chained transport achieves only 11.4%, showing that the gain comes from composing many small high-margin local gauges rather than from using the base model as a trivial reference. In a Llama-2-7B gauge-scramble merge test, signed alignment reduces the peak-above-chord barrier from 6.14 to 0.00, while exact permutation-only alignment is actively harmful. In independently trained 10M-parameter RMSNorm transformers, sign mismatch also appears naturally across seeds, and signed alignment improves the merge barrier from 0.66 to 0.42 over permutation-only alignment. The paper further shows that AdamW optimizer-state transport is gauge-covariant under signed transport: resumed training follows the original trajectory, whereas permutation-only transport diverges despite starting from a functionally equivalent checkpoint. A useful theoretical corollary states that misapplying permutation-only transport to an RMSNorm weight update introduces expected squared error of size 2||ΔW||_F^2, on the same order as the update energy itself.26:T7ce,方法核心是把“语义分析”写成一个 anchor-based coordinate system。给定一组语义锚点 B={b_k}，定义坐标映射 φ，把每层 residual state h_l 映到语义坐标 z_l=φ(h_l)。论文强调，关键不是随便选一个 lens，而是要保证 token interface 在所选 anchor span 上满足 restricted bi-invertibility，也就是 embedding 侧和 readout 侧在这个子空间里足够一致，语义坐标才是稳定可测的。作者给出 pseudo-inverse tying 作为一个 exact anchor-synchronization 的特例：用伪逆把两侧接口对齐，可以得到精确同步的参考系；在更一般情形下，则给出 interface mismatch 导致的 distortion bounds。建立测量条件后，论文把层间演化定义为 semantic trajectory。这里有两个分析对象。其一是连续坐标轨迹：layerwise semantic step、target-direction contribution profile、imbalance measure，用来描述每层往目标语义方向推进了多少、是否存在方向性失衡。其二是离散的 semantic Voronoi trace：由锚点诱导 Voronoi cell，把每个状态分配到“当前最接近/证据最大”的语义锚点，从而得到跨层 cell crossing 的粗粒度路径。作者再引入 margin-relaxed semantic tube，把观测到的 Voronoi trace 变成一条受约束的语义通道，并在其中用 path action 选 minimum-action canonical trace。这样做的目的，是避免只看端点插值，能够区分“路径真的绕了远路”与“测量噪声导致的抖动”。最后，论文证明了若参考系变化足够小，则这些语义动力学量是稳定的；并用 excess action 控制偏离 canonical trace 的程度。进一步，若轨迹曲率能量低，则可以用少量 piecewise-linear knots 压缩整条深度轨迹，作者把这解释为 frame-relative 的 local knowledge density：同样的语义变换，若能用更少的语义折点概括，说明该模型在该任务和参考系下的语义 transport 更紧凑。27:T6f2,The core idea is to define an anchor-based semantic coordinate system. A Semantic Reference Frame consists of a set of semantic anchors and a coordinate map that projects each residual state h_l into semantic coordinates z_l. The main technical requirement is restricted bi-invertibility of the token interface on the chosen anchor span: the embedding-side and readout-side maps must agree sufficiently well on that subspace for semantic coordinates to be stably measurable. The paper presents pseudo-inverse tying as an exact anchor-synchronization case, and in the more general setting derives distortion bounds induced by interface mismatch. Once measurement is controlled, the residual stream can be treated as a semantic trajectory through depth. The paper defines layerwise semantic steps, target-direction contribution profiles, and imbalance diagnostics to quantify how each layer moves the state in semantic space. It then introduces an anchor-induced semantic Voronoi diagram, assigning each hidden state to the anchor with the strongest semantic evidence. This yields a coarse discrete trace of cell crossings. To combine discrete and continuous views, the paper defines a margin-relaxed semantic tube and selects a minimum-action path inside that tube as the canonical trace. The point is to distinguish genuine semantic transport from noisy or frame-induced fluctuations. The framework also includes stability results under nearby admissible frames, excess-action bounds controlling deviations from the canonical trace, and a low-curvature compressibility result: if the semantic trajectory has low curvature energy, it can be summarized by a small number of piecewise-linear knots. The authors interpret this as a local, frame-relative notion of knowledge density.28:T414,A good skim order is: measurement assumptions first, trajectory definitions second, stability claims third. Start with the introduction and the measurement section to understand why the paper treats reference-frame validity as the primary issue in residual-stream analysis. Then read the parts on pseudo-inverse tying, restricted bi-invertibility, and distortion bounds, since these determine when a lens-like readout is semantically trustworthy. After that, read the semantic Voronoi diagram, semantic tube, and canonical trace definitions; this is where the paper moves beyond simple layerwise logit inspection. Finally, look at the low-curvature compressibility and local knowledge density discussion, which is the most speculative but also the most extensible part of the framework. If the goal is only to capture the main idea, the introduction, measurement setup, and canonical-trace sections are enough in roughly 25–35 minutes. Using the framework in practice would require a closer read of the assumptions and stability propositions.29:T7f5,The setup is deliberately narrow and controlled. The authors use the camera-relative direction subset of ViewSpatial-Bench and decompose items into pure horizontal, vertical, and depth axes, which avoids mixing different spatial factors. They evaluate both the native 18-way task accuracy and a within-axis binary accuracy. On top of this task decomposition, they run a standard probe-and-steer pipeline on Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct. A five-fold cross-validated logistic probe is trained on PCA-50 residual-stream features at the decision token, layer by layer, to decode each axis. Steering directions are then extracted either from class mean differences or from back-projected probe weights, and injected as a training-free activation edit in the L20-L24 band, scaled by residual-stream standard deviations and concentrated on top-k neurons with a single global coefficient. Up to this point, the setup mirrors the usual latent-knowledge recovery recipe. The key addition is the vision-ablation arbiter. The image is replaced by a gray blank while keeping the prompt, answer options, and decoding unchanged. The signed gap between real-image and blank-image performance is then used to classify each axis into one of three regimes: grounded-correct if real-image performance is higher and blank performance collapses toward chance; grounded-inverted if real-image performance is below chance but blank performance returns toward chance; prior-driven if real and blank conditions are statistically indistinguishable, including under steering. The paper also includes robustness checks against the objection that a gray blank is an unnatural input, using multiple ablations and mismatched but in-distribution real images. In later sections, the authors analyze the geometry of depth inversion and test minimal corrective edits. Some models can be fixed by a training-free rotation in representation space, while others require a trained low-rank edit, suggesting a spectrum from localized geometric misalignment to more distributed errors.2a:T80a,The headline result comes from the three-axis analysis on Qwen2.5-VL-7B. Behaviorally, horizontal is strong, vertical is near chance, and depth is below chance. Probing tells a very different story: all three axes are decodable from the residual stream, with vertical being the most decodable. The reported within-axis probe accuracies span roughly 73% to 97%, with vertical around 94% and horizontal around 85%. The training-free projection result looks even more convincing under the standard interpretation: vertical task accuracy rises from about 59% to 79%, while horizontal remains largely unchanged, exactly the pattern one would describe as unlocking latent knowledge. The gray-image arbiter overturns that interpretation. Horizontal drops from about 82% with the real image to 49% with the blank, so it is genuinely vision-grounded. Depth rises from 31% with the real image to 45% with the blank, showing that the error requires looking at the image and is therefore grounded but inverted. Vertical stays essentially unchanged, about 59% versus 60%, and the projection gain is almost identical with and without vision, roughly +21 versus +19, indicating that the decoded and amplified signal is a prior rather than visual grounding. The broader sweep covers 14 VLMs from 6 LM families, ranging from 2B to 27B parameters. The recurring pattern is that horizontal tends to be grounded-correct in capable models, vertical is often prior-driven, and depth inversion emerges with scale within families. Another important result is the task boundary: the same models that invert camera-egocentric depth can still be grounded-correct on near-field and metric depth, which argues against a generic lack of depth understanding and points instead to a specific coordinate-mapping failure. In the correction study, depth inversion replicates in 7 of 8 tested models across 5 families. The cleanest model can be corrected by a training-free rotation with performance close to a trained edit, whereas more distributed models need a low-rank trained intervention.2b:T702,The system, AutoTrainess, is an LM agent built on top of a training-specialized Agent-Computer Interface, implemented as AutoTrainHub. Instead of leaving the agent in an open-ended shell, the environment provides a structured workspace and a set of semantically meaningful actions organized into four modules: data processing, training, evaluation, and logging/planning.

In the data module, the workflow is decomposed into data selection, data construction, and data validation. Selection chooses useful directions based on prior failures. Construction operates within a bounded set of transformations such as extraction, cleaning, deduplication, rewriting, restructuring, synthesis, distillation, and schema normalization. Validation explicitly checks compatibility with the benchmark-facing interface, including evaluation scripts and chat templates, and filters low-quality or risky samples. This is an important design choice: many of the checks that human practitioners do implicitly are turned into explicit agent actions.

The training and evaluation modules similarly expose actions such as launching training, launching evaluation, debugging evaluation, and summarizing results, all tied to scripts and artifacts in the workspace. The logging/planning module maintains experiment memory and supports iterative decision-making for the next round. So AutoTrainess is not a one-shot executor; it is an iterative post-training loop with structured state.

Methodologically, this is best understood as an agent harness or environment design paper. The claim is that for training tasks, good action abstraction matters more than unrestricted CLI freedom because it encodes human prior knowledge directly into the callable interface and reduces both search complexity and low-level mistakes.2c:T419,The main evaluation is on PostTrainBench. With GPT-5.4 (Codex) as the backbone, AutoTrainess achieves an average score of 26.94, compared with 23.21 for a CLI-only baseline. On the Qwen3-4B subset of PostTrainBench, ablations show that the training-specialized ACI contributes roughly 3–8 points. To test portability, the authors replace the backbone with DeepSeek-V4-Flash (OpenCode); AutoTrainess still reaches 19.58, well above the CLI-only baseline of 12.13, while maintaining stable end-to-end training with minimal human oversight.

These results support two claims. First, the gain is not merely due to a stronger backbone, since the same backbone improves when given the specialized interface. Second, the interface design is at least somewhat transferable across agent backbones. If the full paper includes task-level breakdowns, failure-type statistics, job success rates, or data-construction error analyses, those are especially worth reading because they reveal whether the ACI truly reduces the fragile points in autonomous training.3:["$","div",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-4xl px-4 py-8 sm:px-6 xl:max-w-6xl","children":["$","$L9",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-07-02","generated_at":"2026-07-02T00:55:59.673711+00:00","stats":{"total":101,"starred":2,"must_read":5,"skim":47,"glance":49,"by_category":{"pretraining_data":2,"llm_pretrain":1,"scaling":1,"architecture":6,"efficient":8,"eval":15,"multimodal":11,"multimodal_gen":8,"post_train":18,"mechanism":15,"coding_agent":1,"agent":15},"trending_repos":10,"trending_hf":10,"fetch_status":{"arxiv":"ok","huggingface":"failed","blog":"ok","twitter":"empty","github_trending":"ok","hf_trending":"ok"},"fetch_errors":{"huggingface":"exhausted 5 retries; last error: Client error '400 Bad Request' for url 'https://huggingface.co/api/daily_papers?date=2026-07-02'\nFor more information check: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Status/400"}},"categories":[{"id":"pretraining_data","emoji":"📚","name":"预训练数据","description":"以预训练数据为核心贡献的工作：数据采集/清洗/去重、数据配比(data mixture)、数据筛选/质量评估、语料构建、数据课程(data curriculum)、数据重复(repetition)、合成数据用于预训练、Tokenizer/词表设计、成员推断/数据检测。判据是『论文核心是数据本身』，而非训练 recipe/优化器(→ llm_pretrain)或评测资产(→ eval)","papers":[{"id":"2606.30875","title":"The Label Imitation Game: Turing Test Network for Zero-Shot Pseudo-Label Pruning","abstract":"Foundation model pseudo-labeling - labeling data strictly via zero-shot inference - enables massive scale, but performance is undermined by hallucinations that evade standard thresholds. Transformers and ResNets develop structured relationships across depth during training, but standard optimizers still construct and apply per-layer updates largely independently.","importance":"The main relevance for pretraining is that it turns layer coupling from an analysis observation into an optimizer-side intervention. The method does not change the architecture and can be placed after common optimizers such as Adam, AdamW, SGD, or Muon. This makes it engineering-light compared with architectural changes. For large-scale pretraining, the open question is whether the reported gains survive at larger model and token scales, especially under tightly tuned optimizer recipes.","technical_approach":"The paper introduces Depth-wise Gradient Augmentation. A base optimizer first produces block-wise updates for all repeated layers. A depth-wise operator then transforms this collection of updates before applying them to the model. The concrete instantiation is Gradient Smoothing, with Window Smoothing as the simplest operator: each layer receives a local average of optimizer updates from neighboring layers. The smoothing is applied to optimizer updates rather than raw gradients, which is why the method can be composed with adaptive optimizers and matrix-based optimizers. Conceptually, it behaves like a structured preconditioner over a block-structured parameter space, using the assumption that nearby layers often follow related learning dynamics.","experiments":"The reported experiments span language model pretraining, RL fine-tuning of LLMs for reasoning, supervised Vision Transformer training, and diffusion model training. Then inspect the language model pretraining and RL fine-tuning experiments, since these are closest to foundation-model training. The representation analysis is worth reading to see whether alignment metrics plausibly explain the optimization gains. empiri","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-29T18:19:09Z","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/2606_30789.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","stat.ML"],"summary":{"core_problem":"Predictable GRPO 解决的是 GRPO 训练动态缺少可解释模型的问题。现有做法通常把 reward curve 拟合成简单指数曲线，但拟合常数没有机制含义，学习率、momentum、group size、reference refresh interval 等超参仍主要靠试错。论文试图从 GRPO size 对确定性 reward trajectory 的影响和对噪声方差的影响被分开；第二，refresh lag、momentum、learning rate 的稳定性作用被写成可检验条件；第三，reward curve 之外的诊断可以区分 mean-field 假设失效、advantage degeneracy、policy concentration 和真实动力学不稳定。这些结论对大规模 analysis 对离散优化过程做二阶截断，得到一个带随机驱动的阻尼振子方程。模型中的 effective mass、damping、stiffness 不是自由拟合参数，而由优化器超参和一个可测曲率尺度闭式决定。论文给出的机制解释很清楚：momentum 把一阶梯度流提升成带惯性的二阶系统；off-policy lag 或 reference refresh interval 会引入惯性，同时侵蚀阻尼；group size 主要进入噪声温度，而不改变确定性轨迹；曲率决定回到固定点的刚度。已有经验中的单指数 reward saturation law 被解释为该动力学的过阻尼极限。保留惯性项后，模型可以表达早期 slow-start inflection，这是单指数形式覆盖不了的部分。论文还给出几个可检验预测，包括确定性轨迹对 group size 近似不变、稳态波动随 p。Theorem 7 是核心，给出随机阻尼振子形式以及 mass、damping、stiffness 的来源。Lemma 4 到 Lemma 6 分别对应 momentum、off-policy lag、group size 的作用，适合和实际训练超参逐项对照。Corollary 13 和 Proposition 14 解释经验 saturation law 与 slow-start mostly empirical. Current practice often fits reward trajectories with simple exponential laws, but the fitted constants do not explain how learning rate, momentum, group size, KL anchoring, and refresh interval shape reward growth, saturation, variance, and instability. The paper derives a closed-form reduced-order model directly from the GRPO update.","importance":"The main value is a mechanistic view of RL post-training dynamics. The paper does not introduce a new optimizer; it turns GRPO training into a measurable dynamical system. This is useful for large-scale LLM training because it separates deterministic reward progress from sampling noise, explains why larger group size may reduce fluctuations without changing the mean trajectory, and gives stability predictions tied to optimizer settings and a measured curvature scale. It also provides diagnostics beyond the reward curve, helping distinguish mean-field breakdown, advantage degeneracy, policy concentration, and true dynamical instability.","technical_approach":"$a","experiments":"The empirical validation has two parts. On real GRPO runs, across three models and two group sizes, the proposed three-phase law fits training reward with R² ≥ 0.91. The predicted group-size invariance is observed both in the mean reward trajectory and in transfer to eight out-of-distribution benchmarks: changing group size mainly affects fluctuation scale, not the deterministic path. For stability and oscillation predictions, the paper uses a controlled softmax-bandit reduction where the mean-field assumption holds exactly. This setting reproduces the overdamped-to-oscillatory transition and locates the refresh-interval threshold using an independently measured stiffness. The limitation is explicit: the deep-network GSM8K runs are overdamped, so a direct large-model demonstration of the oscillatory transition is left for future work.","reading_guide":"Read carefully. Start with the formal GRPO setup to track the roles of momentum, KL anchoring, group sampling, and policy refresh. Then focus on the mean-field assumption, because it is the main modeling compression. Theorem 7 is the central result: it maps GRPO to a forced damped oscillator with closed-form coefficients. Lemmas 4–6 are the most useful for practitioners, since they isolate the effects of momentum, off-policy lag, and group size. Corollary 13 and Proposition 14 explain how the empirical exponential saturation law emerges and why an inertial model captures slow-start behavior. Proposition 9 and Proposition 10 are the most actionable predictions for hyperparameter sweeps. In the experiments, prioritize the reward-curve fits, the group-size comparison, the OOD transfer results, and the softmax-bandit exact reduction in Section 5.6. Latent CoT offers an alternative: it performs multi-step reasoning in the model's hidden states, replacing decoded tokens with continuous representations for greater efficiency. However, existing latent CoT methods underperform explicit CoT beyond 1B parameters, and the gap widens with scale. Looped, or recurrent-depth, Transformers, which reuse their weights to increase computatio","starred":true,"priority":"p1","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-30T14:58:53Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2606_31779.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作解决的是大模型 latent reasoning 里最关键的两个断点：一是许多 latent CoT 方法仍然按自回归方式逐个生成 latent token，顺序步数仍随推理长度线性增长；二是 latent trace 缺少像显式 CoT 那样逐位置、逐 token 的直接监督，规模变大后容易和可解释的推理过程脱钩。目标不是简单压缩 CoT，而是在 3B 级别模型上，让连续隐状态推理接近显式 CoT 的准确率，同时显著减少 thought phase 的串行延迟。","importance":"价值在于把 latent reasoning 的失败点拆得比较清楚，并给出一个可扩展的结构化解法。LOTUS 说明，latent CoT 要在 billion-scale 上工作，不能只依赖隐状态蒸馏、KV 压缩或间接答案监督；它需要足够直接的 CoT token grounding，也需要把中间推理从自回归序列改成并行可细化的计算。对预训练研究的启发主要在两个方向：第一，looped / recurrent-depth Transformer 可以作为 inference compute scaling 的参数高效载体；第二，隐藏状态如果要承担推理过程，训练目标需要让它和语言空间保持可读、可校验的对应关系。这比单纯追求不可见 latent efficiency 更稳。","technical_approach":"方法叫 LOTUS，即 Looped Transformers with parallel supervision on latents。它在问题和答案之间插入 K 个可学习的 padded latent blocks，每个 block 含 c 个 latent tokens。整段序列经过同一个 Transformer backbone 重复处理 R 次，权重共享，用 recurrent depth 增加计算量。这样，一个长度为 N 的显式 CoT 不再需要 N 次逐 token 解码，而是用 R 次并行 dense computation 来细化 latent slots，且 R 远小于 N。\n\n关键训练信号是 parallel cross-entropy supervision。每个 post-loop latent position 通过 base model 自带的 LM head 去预测对应的 gold CoT token。也就是说，latent slot 不是只为了最后答案服务，而是被直接拉到显式 CoT 的 token 空间里。这个设计和 Coconut、CODI、SIM-CoT 的差别在于：它不再逐步自回归生成 latent thoughts。和 PCCoT、KaVa 这类 Jacobi-style 或 KV 压缩路线的差别在于：它不主要依赖 teacher hidden states 或压缩 cache 这类间接目标，而是用 gold CoT token 做位置对齐监督。\n\n作者还实现了 LOTUS-aux。它把同样的 latent supervision 路由到一个辅助 decoder：decoder 条件化在所有 latents 上，并在 teacher forcing 下给 gold CoT tokens 打分。辅助 decoder 只用于训练。结果显示，aux 路线在 3B 上可以接近 direct LM-head routing，但在小模型上更容易退化。这个现象说明，直接使用 base LM head 做 latent-to-token grounding 更稳，可能因为它避免了额外 decoder 学到一套和主干不完全一致的读出空间。","experiments":"核心实验使用 Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct。作者报告 LOTUS 在 GSM8K test 上把 latent reasoning 和显式 CoT 的差距缩到 1 个点以内，并且在 out-of-domain math 平均指标上超过显式 CoT。延迟上，math-expression setting 的 thought-phase latency 降低 2.5 倍；在更啰嗦的自然语言 CoT stress test 上，准确率与显式 CoT 持平，同时 thought-phase latency 降低 6.9 倍。\n\n消融实验给出两个比较实用的结论。第一，parallel supervision 很关键。没有直接、位置对齐的 CoT token 监督，latent traces 更难稳定地承载推理过程。第二，looped architecture 的容量配置也很关键，需要足够的 block width 和 loop depth。只把普通 Transformer 换成 latent slots，或只做浅层循环，都不足以填平显式 CoT 的差距。\n\n可解释性实验也有信息量。作者把 post-loop latents 直接通过 base LM head 读出，能够恢复大部分 gold intermediate tokens，并且有时会给出训练目标中没有出现、但逻辑上有效的替代中间步骤。这说明 latent space 不是完全黑箱的连续压缩，而是和语言化推理步骤保持了较强对应关系。","reading_guide":"建议精读 Section 3.1 到 3.4。重点看 padded latent prefix 如何插入序列、looped backbone 如何重复处理、latent positions 如何和 gold CoT tokens 对齐，以及 LOTUS-aux 为什么只在训练时使用。\n\n实验部分优先看 Figure 1、Section 4.2、Section 4.3.1 和 Section 4.3.2。Figure 1 给出 latent CoT 和显式 CoT 在 3B 上的主结果。Section 4.2 需要关注准确率与 CoT，而不只是对答案有用。对于预训练研究者，最值得深挖的是三个问题：latent slots 和 CoT tokens 的长度错配如何处理；R 次 loop 的 compute 与显式 CoT token 解码的 wall-clock 比较是否公平；如果把这种目标前移到 mid-training 或 reasoning-oriented continual Many latent CoT methods still generate latent thoughts autoregressively, so the number of sequential forward passes grows with the reasoning length. At the same time, their latent traces are often weakly grounded: unlike explicit CoT, they do not receive direct, position-aligned token supervision for each reasoning step. Drawing on the human-factors principle of independent verification, we introduce Review Residuals, which scale each update by a learned, input-dependent gate conditioned on both the current state and the proposed update: h_l = h_{l-1} + r_l * u_l with r_l = sigmoid(W[RMSNorm(h_{l-1}), RMSNorm(u_l)]). whether it should learn an input-dependent gate that evaluates the proposed update before adding it.","importance":"The work is relevant mainly as a residual-stream architecture study. The empirical evidence is limited to TinyStories and models up to 1B parameters, and the reported loss gains are small. Still, the question is useful for pretraining research: deep Transformers need both a stable identity path and some mechanism for controlling when a sublayer update should be trusted. This connects to residual scaling, gated residuals, depth stability, and training dynamics in large decoder-only models.","technical_approach":"Review Residuals use an additive gated update: h_l = h_{l-1} + r_l ⊙ u_l. The proposed update u_l is produced by the sublayer from RMSNorm(h_{l-1}). The gate r_l is per-channel and is computed as sigmoid(W [RMSNorm(h_{l-1}), RMSNorm(u_l)]). The gate matrix is zero-initialized, so the initial gate value is about 0.5.\n\nThe distinctive feature is update-conditioning: the gate reads the update that it is about to modulate, rather than only reading the current residual state. The paper also makes a useful stability point. A Highway-style convex gate, h_l = (1-r_l) h_{l-1} + r_l u_l, weakens the identity path. If r is near 0.5, gradients decay roughly like 0.5^L across depth. The additive form keeps the coefficient on h_{l-1} equal to one and preserves the main benefit of residual connections.","experiments":"The experiments train GPT-2-like decoder-only Transformers from scratch on TinyStories with RMSNorm, GELU MLPs, and tied embeddings. Training uses fp32, TF32 matmuls, AdamW, and a warmup-then-cosine schedule. Baselines are parameter-matched by widening: a standard residual model and a Highway-gated model.\n\nThe model sizes are 60M, 150M, 320M, 590M, and 1B, with 3, 3, 2, 3, and 3 seeds respectively. At small scale, Review Residuals do not help; at 60M, the parameter-matched standard residual is slightly better. The convex Highway gate fails at depth, with the loss stalling around 6.0 in the 590M and 1B settings. At 590M, Review improves validation loss by 0.009 nats over Highway gating with t=3.3, and by 0.011 nats over the standard residual with t=3.7, both passing p<0.05. At 1B, the gain is about 0.016 nats over both baselines, with t≈2.3 and p≈0.07.","reading_guide":"Skim in about 20–30 minutes. The training objective is then developed through a variational lower bound on log likelihood. Diffusion, score matching, and flow matching are presented as different parameterizations of this general objective. ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-30T08:24:05Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2606_31315.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是 diffusion-based speculative decoding 里一个此前被当作静态超参数的问题：block size 应不应该对所有样本固定。现有方法通常沿用训练时的固定 block size，在推理时对所有输入一刀切；但全文的实验表明，不同样本的最优 block size 差异明显，而且这个选择会直接影响 acceptance length、target model 验证开销和最终 wall-clock speedup。论文要解决的核心问题不是再造一个更强的 draft model，而是在不改 target / draft / verifier 主体框架的前提下，学习一个样本自适应的 decoding policy，让每个输入在 prefilling 之后选择更合适的并行生成粒度。","importance":"对基座研究员来说，价值主要不在 diffusion draft 本身，而在一个更普遍的结论：推理策略也是可学习对象，不一定只能靠离线手调。这里作者把“固定并行度”改写成“条件化策略选择”，而且证明最优动作分布具有局部性，因而可以用很轻的分类器近似。这个思路对 speculative decoding、early exit、adaptive compute、dynamic depth 甚至 chunked generation 都有启发。量级上，它不是改写范式的工作，但属于把已有高性能推理框架再往前推一步的实用改进，尤其适合已经部署 DFlash 一类 block diffusion speculative decoding 的系统。","technical_approach":"方法核心是 BlockPilot，一个在 prefilling 结束后只调用一次的 block-size policy predictor。作者先做了系统性的 block size sweep，发现样本级最优 block size 并不固定，但大多集中在训练 block size 附近，而不是在整个大动作空间里发散。基于这个观察，作者把问题定义成局部离散动作空间上的分类，而不是在线搜索或强化学习。\n\n状态表示方面，BlockPilot 不读取整段 hidden states，也不引入额外的复杂序列编码器，而是直接使用 target model 在 prefilling 后“最后一个 token 的预测分布”作为当前解码状态表征。作者的理由是，这个分布已经通过自回归注意力聚合了完整上下文，既反映当前不确定性，也隐含后续 continuation 的稳定性，因此能预测可接受的并行长度。动作空间则围绕训练 block size 构造一个局部候选集合，避免大范围搜索带来的标注和部署成本。\n\n从系统集成角度，这个设计很克制：不改 target model，不改 diffusion draft，不改 verification 机制，只是在正式解码前增加一次轻量决策。也就是说，它学习的是“用什么 block size 跑现有框架”，而不是“重新训练一个新的 speculative decoder”。这和很多通过增强 draft quality 来提速的方法不同，BlockPilot 把增益来源放在 policy selection 上。\n\n从全文叙述看，作者还强调了 locality 的方法论意义：如果最优 block size 在全局上高度离散，策略学习会退化成高噪声的难问题；但当最优值集中在训练点附近时，问题就更像 calibration + routing。这个假设是方法成立的关键，也解释了为什么一个轻量 predictor 就能工作。","experiments":"实验重点有三层。第一层是现象验证：作者在多个代表性数据集上对不同 block size 做 sweep，证明固定策略与样本最优策略之间存在稳定 gap，而且默认训练 block size 只对一部分样本最优。第二层是分布分析：最优 block size 呈现明显局部结构，集中在训练配置附近，这为局部分类器提供了依据。第三层是端到端收益：在 Qwen3-4B、temperature=1 的设置下，BlockPilot 达到 5.92 的 acceptance length 和 4.20× speedup。按摘要和引言给出的表述，这个结果是在现有 diffusion-based speculative decoding 框架上进一步提升，而不是替换底层方法。\n\n从应关注的实验维度看，最关键的不是单个 speedup 数字，而是：1）不同数据集/任务上 block-size 最优值的方差有多大；2）policy predictor 的额外开销是否足够小，能否被 speedup 增益覆盖；3）在不同 temperature 下 acceptance 与速度的关系是否稳定；4）局部动作空间如果放宽或收窄，性能如何变化。如果正文里给了 predictor 输入特征、类别数、训练标签构造方式和错误案例，这些部分比主表更值得看，因为它们决定方法是否能迁移到别的 speculative decoding 框架。","reading_guide":"泛读建议是先看引言里的问题设定和 Fig.1/2，确认它工作的边界：它不是新的 diffusion LM，也不是新的 verifier，而是给现有 block diffusion speculative decoding 加一个自适应策略层。然后重点看 block size sweep 和最优 block size 分布图，这部分决定整篇论文是否成立。若这些图显示 locality 很强，后面的分类建模就顺理成章；若 locality 弱，方法的普适性就会打折。\n\n接着看方法部分里状态表示和动作空间设计。重点判断两个问题：为什么最后一个 token 的 predictive distribution 足够；为什么只做一次 prefilling 后决策，而不是在线逐步调整。最后看主结果表和消融，尤其是 predictor 开销、不同 block 候选集、不同模型/数据集迁移性。预计 20–30 分钟可以完成有效泛读；若你在做 speculative decoding 或 adaptive inference，建议把实验部分连同附录一起看完。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies a neglected control variable in diffusion-based speculative decoding: whether block size should remain fixed for all inputs at inference time. Existing methods typically inherit a single block size from training and apply it uniformly, but the full paper argues that this is suboptimal because the best block size varies across samples and strongly affects acceptance length, verification cost, and end-to-end speedup. The core problem is therefore not to build a better draft model, but to learn an input-adaptive decoding policy that selects a better parallel generation granularity after prefilling, without changing the target model, the diffusion draft model, or the verifier.","importance":"For pretraining and systems researchers, the main value is the broader lesson that inference policy can itself be learned rather than hand-tuned as a static hyperparameter. The paper reframes fixed parallelism as conditional policy selection, and shows that the optimal action distribution has a local structure, which makes lightweight prediction feasible. That idea is relevant beyond diffusion speculative decoding: it connects to adaptive compute, dynamic depth, early exit, and other routing-style inference decisions. This is not a paradigm-shifting modeling paper, but it is a practical systems improvement that can matter if one already uses block-diffusion speculative decoding frameworks such as DFlash.","technical_approach":"$d","experiments":"$e","reading_guide":"Start with the introduction and the first figures to pin down the scope: this is not a new diffusion LM or a new verifier, but an adaptive policy layer for existing block-diffusion speculative decoding. Then go directly to the block-size sweep results and the plots of optimal block-size distributions. Those figures are the foundation of the paper. If the locality pattern is strong, the classification formulation is justified; if not, the method becomes much less convincing.\n\nNext, read the method section with two questions in mind: why is the final-token predictive distribution a sufficient state signal, and why is a one-shot decision after prefilling preferable to online adjustment during decoding? Finish with the main results and ablations, especially predictor overhead, candidate action sets, and transfer across models or datasets. A useful skim should take about 20–30 minutes. If you work on speculative decoding or adaptive inference, the appendix is likely worth reading as well.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这类工作不改 pretrain，但会影响你对离散 diffusion 解码瓶颈的判断；只看自适应 block size 的证据和收益曲线，别把它误读成新建模范式。","en":"It does not change pretraining, but it sharpens where diffusion decoding actually bottlenecks; read the evidence for adaptive block sizing and the speedup curves, not as a new LM paradigm."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Hao Zhang","Yiming Hu","Yong Wang","Mingqiao Mo","Xin Xiao","Xiangxiang Chu"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["speculative-decoding","diffusion-decoding","inference-optimization","adaptive-policy"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31315","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.31315","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2606.31519","title":"RaBitQCache: Rotated Binary Quantization for KVCache in Long Context LLM Inference","abstract":"Long-context Large Language Model inference is severely bottlenecked by the massive Key-Value (KV) cache, yet existing sparse attention methods often suffer from static fixed-budget (Top-k) retrieval or rely on proxy scores that are computationally expensive and biased. To address these limitations, we propose RaBitQCache, a novel sparse attention framework that utilizes randomized rotated binary quantization and high-throughput binary-INT4 arithmetic to efficiently estimate attention weights. O","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-30T11:32:14Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2606_31519.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是长上下文推理里的 KV cache 访问瓶颈。问题不只是显存大，而是每一步解码都要在巨大的历史 KV 上做选择和读取。已有 sparse attention / KV offloading 方法通常依赖固定预算的 Top-k 检索，或者依赖近似但偏差较大的 proxy score 来估计注意力重要性。前者不够自适应，后者既不够准，也缺少误差保证，因此很难安全地做 Top-p 这类按真实注意力质量自适应分配预算的检索。作者要解决的核心问题是：能否构造一个足够便宜、足够准、还有理论误差界的注意力代理分数，用它在不访问完整 KV cache 的前提下做动态稀疏检索。","importance":"这篇论文属于 efficient inference，而不是预训练本身，但对大模型系统研究有现实价值。它的重要性主要在两个层面。第一，方法上它把 KV 选择问题明确地写成高维向量相似度估计问题，并借助 Johnson-Lindenstrauss 视角构造了带理论界的 proxy score，这比很多经验型 sparse attention 方法更扎实。第二，系统上它不是只给一个近似公式，而是把二值化、INT4 查询、异步流水和 lazy update 一起做成了可运行方案。对基座研究员来说，直接启发不在建模，而在于：长上下文能力的上限越来越受 memory movement 和 retrieval policy 约束，未来训练和推理可能需要更统一地考虑“哪些历史 token 值得被精确保留”。","technical_approach":"方法名是 RaBitQCache，核心思想是把 Key 的高维浮点向量映射成随机旋转后的二值码，再用一个极轻量的二值-低比特算术近似 query-key 内积，从而估计注意力权重。具体分成几个层次。\n\n第一层是表示压缩。在 prefill 阶段，作者对 Key 向量做随机旋转，然后映射到单位超立方体顶点，也就是得到 binary code。随机旋转的作用是让原始空间中的几何关系在低成本表示里尽量保真，避免直接按坐标符号切分带来的偏置。这个设计明显受 JL lemma 和相关近似近邻检索方法启发。\n\n第二层是 proxy score 构造。在 decoding 阶段，不再直接用全精度 query 与所有 key 做内积，而是把 Query 做 INT4 量化，再与二值化 Key 进行高吞吐计算，得到一个对真实 attention score 的无偏估计。论文强调两点：一，这个估计器是 unbiased；二，它有严格的误差界。前者意味着估计值不会系统性高估或低估某类 token，后者意味着不仅能做相对排序，还能对分数大小本身有一定可信度，这正是支持 Top-p 检索的关键。\n\n第三层是检索策略。不同于固定 Top-k，RaBitQCache 用估计到的注意力质量做 adaptive Top-p retrieval。因为不同层、不同头、不同任务的注意力稀疏度差异很大，固定 k 往往要么取多了浪费算力，要么取少了伤精度。Top-p 更接近“保留足够多的重要质量”，理论上更适合异质的注意力分布，但前提就是 proxy score 必须能近似真实权重幅值，而不只是给个粗排序。\n\n第四层是系统实现。作者实现了硬件感知的 kernel 和调度，包括异步流水线以及 lazy updates，用来隐藏二值检索和数据搬运的额外开销。这个部分很关键，因为很多 sparse/offloading 方法算法上省 FLOPs，系统上却被 gather/scatter 和 CPU-GPU 传输拖垮。RaBitQCache 试图把“近似检索”真正做成吞吐友好的路径。\n\n和 Quest、DS 这类方法相比，区别主要在三点：检索粒度更细，不是整页粗选；proxy score 更接近真实内积，不是部分维度或启发式统计；支持自适应 Top-p，而不是被迫固定预算。","experiments":"从引言和方法描述看，作者做的是长上下文推理加速实验，并声称显著优于现有 sparse attention / KV offloading 方法。虽然给出的片段没有完整主表数字，但正文明确强调了几个结果方向。第一，在不访问完整 KV cache 的前提下，RaBitQCache 能在保持模型性能基本稳定的同时提升长上下文推理效率。第二，相比现有方法，它的优势来自更准确的 token 重要性估计，因此在相同预算下精度损失更小，或者在相近精度下可进一步减少检索 token 数。第三，系统优化后，额外的代理分数计算和调度开销被较好掩蔽，否则算法收益很难兑现成端到端 latency 改善。\n\n如果通读全文，建议重点抓几类实验。其一，proxy score 与真实 attention score 的相关性或误差分析，这是方法成立的根。其二，Top-k 与 Top-p 的对比，尤其是在不同层、不同头、不同任务上的预算自适应效果。其三，和 Quest、DS、静态位置选择等基线的端到端 latency、吞吐、显存/带宽占用比较。其四，消融随机旋转、二值码长度、INT4 量化精度、lazy update 和异步流水各自贡献。真正有说服力的结果通常不是单一 speedup，而是 accuracy-latency 曲线整体外移。","reading_guide":"泛读建议 25 到 35 分钟。先看引言里对现有动态 KV 检索两类缺陷的归纳：proxy 不准，以及没有误差界。这两点决定了作者为什么坚持做带理论保证的估计器。然后直接看方法部分中 proxy score 的定义与定理，确认无偏性和误差界到底针对的是 query-key 内积、attention logits，还是归一化后的权重。接着看系统实现图，判断异步流水和 lazy update 是否只是工程补丁，还是和算法设计强耦合。最后看主实验和消融，重点关注 accuracy-latency tradeoff，而不是只看单点加速比。\n\n如果只想快速判断价值，优先读三处：理论部分的主命题、Top-p long-context decoding: not just the size of the KV cache, but the cost of repeatedly selecting and reading from a very large history. Existing sparse-attention and KV-offloading methods usually rely on either fixed-budget Top-k retrieval or cheap but biased proxy scores for token importance. Fixed Top-k is not adaptive enough, while weak proxies are often inaccurate and lack error guarantees, which makes adaptive Top-p retrieval hard to trust. The core question here is whether one can build a proxy score that is cheap, accurate, and theoretically controlled, so that dynamic sparse retrieval can be done without touching the full KV cache.","importance":"This is an inference-efficiency paper rather than a pretraining paper, but it is relevant to foundation-model systems work. Its main value is twofold. First, it formulates KV selection as a high-dimensional similarity estimation problem and brings in a Johnson-Lindenstrauss-style perspective to construct a proxy with explicit guarantees. That is methodologically stronger than many heuristic sparse-attention designs. Second, it does not stop at the estimator: it also builds a hardware-aware implementation around binary keys, INT4 queries, asynchronous pipelining, and lazy updates. Its logit rectification scheme is compatible with optimized attention implementations such as FlashAttention, making it deployment-friendly and directly integratable into mainstream inference frameworks like vLLM.","technical_approach":"The ERA framework consists of three synergistic components: 1. Dual-view Entropy Pruning (DEP): jointly models visual diversity and head-wise attention entropy to select anchor tokens that cover both complementary visual patterns and head-specific critical evidence. 2. Bias-aware Token Recycling (BTR): aggregates pruned tokens to their nearest anchors, while estimating cluster-level accumulated attention logit biases to preserve the attention contribution of discarded tokens. 3. Logit-preserving Attention Rectification (LAR): injects the estimated cluster biases into attention logits to correct distribution shifts caused by token reduction; it is implemented via matrix augmentation and remains fully compatible with efficient attention implementations like FlashAttention.","experiments":"Tested across three scenarios (single-image, multi-image, video) on representative MLLMs with different visual token generation paradigms.
