tasks: networks fit the training set early, but test accuracy remains poor for a long time before a sharp transition. The proposed explanation is radial inflation of hidden representations under cross-entropy, which favors high-norm memorizing solutions before structured low-dimensional circuits are assembled.","importance":"This is not a large-scale LLM pretraining paper, but it is useful for understanding training dynamics. Its main contribution is a measurable geometric lens: decompose representation updates into radial and angular components. For pretraining research, the relevant message is that cross-entropy can drive activation norm growth, not just better prediction. That growth can interact with feature formation, effective rank, curvature, and late-emerging generalization. The result should be treated as evidence from controlled algorithmic settings rather than as a direct prescription for web-scale language modeling.","technical_approach":"The intervention is a centered activation norm penalty with target radius sqrt(d), where d is the hidden width. The target keeps average squared activation per feature stable as width changes, while preventing unbounded norm growth. In MLPs, the penalty is applied to pre-activation hidden outputs. In Pre-LayerNorm Transformers, it is applied to each sublayer output before it is added to the residual stream, so it constrains the increment rather than the accumulated residual state. The analysis decomposes gradients into radial and tangential components and introduces normalized fractional radial energy. The paper then derives three testable predictions: the penalty behaves like a data-dependent anisotropic weight regularizer, it suppresses radial gradient energy below an isotropic random baseline, and it biases training toward flatter solutions.","experiments":"The empirical study uses algorithmic generalization tasks, including modular arithmetic, across MLPs, Transformers, and a 10M-parameter nanoGPT. Several numbers are notable. The penalty drives normalized radial energy to about 0.15 from initialization, well below the isotropic baseline of 1. On the P=97 Fourier basis, Fourier coherence with R^2>0.9 is reached at epoch 2,460 instead of 34,200 for the baseline, and the dominant Fourier magnitude increases by about 4x. Grokking onset is about 6.3x faster than strong isotropic weight decay and about 6.1x faster than MAN. The effective rank is also higher than under strong weight decay, reported as 443 versus 402. The speedup on nanoGPT is smaller, which the authors attribute to LayerNorm already providing partial radial suppression.","reading_guide":"A useful skim takes around 30 to 40 minutes. AutoTrainHub 的结构化工作区和动作库，把自主 post-training 组织成四个闭环模块：data process、training、evaluation、logging & planning。\n\n数据处理模块里，作者把动作拆成 data selection、data construction、data validation。selection 负责根据上一轮失败案例选择数据来源和方向；construction 允许在受限操作集内做抽取、清洗、去重、重写、重组、合成、蒸馏、schema normalization；validation 则要求代理先检查 benchmark 的真实评测接口、chat template 和任务上下文，再过滤垃圾样本、损坏样本、重复样本和不符合接口的样本。这个设计的关键不是“功能多”，而是把人类训练时默认会做的检查前置成显式动作。\n\n训练与评测模块则把 run train.sh、run eval.sh、debug evaluation、summarize evaluation results 这类操作语义化，并与工作区中的脚本、日志和计划文件绑定。日志与规划模块要求代理记录每轮实验状态，并基于已有证据决定下一轮方向。也就是说，AutoTrainess 不是一个单步执行器，而是一个带实验记忆的迭代系统。\n\n从方法定位看，它更像 agent harness / environment design，而不是新的学习算法。作者的主张是：在训练任务上，好的 action abstraction 比开放式 CLI 更重要，因为它把人类经验编码进了 agent 可调用的接口里，减少了搜索空间和低级错误。这个思路与软件工程 agent 中的 tool-use 很像，但这里工具不是通用 IDE，而是训练流程专用接口。","experiments":"实验主要在 PostTrainBench 上进行。使用 GPT-5.4（Codex）作为 backbone 时，AutoTrainess 平均分 26.94，高于 CLI-only baseline 的 23.21。作者还在 PostTrainBench 的 Qwen3-4B 子集上做了消融，显示训练专用 ACI 带来约 3–8 分提升。为了验证接口设计不是只对单一 backbone 有效，作者又换成 DeepSeek-V4-Flash（OpenCode），AutoTrainess 仍达到 19.58，显著高于 CLI-only 的 12.13，同时能在较少人工干预下保持稳定的端到端训练流程。\n\n这些结果说明两件事。第一，增益不是来自更强 backbone 本身，因为同一 backbone 下 ACI 相比裸 CLI 有稳定提升。第二，接口设计具有一定可移植性，不完全绑定某个闭源模型。若全文提供了任务分项、失败类型统计、训练作业成功率、数据构造错误率或评测脚本调试次数，这些会比平均分更能说明方法价值，因为它们直接反映 ACI 是否减少了训练流程中的脆弱点。","reading_guide":"泛读时先看引言和 Figure 2，理解作者到底在改什么：不是让 agent 学会新的优化器或 子集上的消融。阅读时要特别留意 benchmark 评分到底覆盖哪些能力，是否真正衡量了多轮 post-training 闭环，而不只是脚本执行成功。预计 20–30 分钟可以完成有效泛读。如果你在做 autonomous training、research agent 或 post-train platform，这篇值得连附录一起看，重点找失败案例和接口定义细节。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks why autonomous LM improvement remains difficult even for strong coding agents, and argues that the bottleneck is not just coding ability but the lack of a training-aware interface. End-to-end post-training requires repeated planning, benchmark-aligned data construction, stable training execution, evaluation, and experiment-state management over many iterations. In a raw CLI environment, agents frequently fail on details such as sequence packing, chat templates, evaluation scripts, and bookkeeping. The core problem is therefore how to expose human training expertise to an agent in a structured, operational form.","importance":"The main contribution is conceptual and infrastructural rather than algorithmic. For researchers working on pretrain/post-train loops, continual improvement, or autonomous research systems, this is useful because it shows where automation actually breaks in practice. The paper does not introduce a new optimization method, but it offers a concrete recipe for packaging training heuristics, constraints, and workflow structure into an agent-computer interface.","technical_approach":"$2b","experiments":"$2c","reading_guide":"Start with the introduction and Figure 2 to understand the scope clearly: this is not a new optimizer or a new RL method for training LMs, but a structured interface for autonomous post-training. the agent retrieves the right value, it still has to reconstruct the current workflow state (what is done, what is pending, what is already used, what action is valid now). If interaction trajectories are used in continual pretraining or mid-training, the supervision should likely shift from raw histories to structured state updates and action validity signals.","technical_approach":"Active Task-Driving Memory (ATMem) reframes memory as an explicit, hierarchical execution state rather than a chronological note or a fixed hand-engineered tracker. ATMem contains: (1) Workflow Progress (phase + which app-level files/objects are completed/pending), (2) Constraints (instruction logic encoded independently from schema), (3) Schema (minimal task-relevant fields and properties), and (4) ItemContent (observed instance values plus execution status such as pending/ready/used/verified). The agent updates ATMem after each observation/outcome and conditions action selection on ATMem + current GUI observation + history.\nTraining is two-stage: SFT on verified trajectories to learn to construct/update ATMem and reference it for action prediction; then STR-GRPO, an online RL method intended to optimize the policy of using ATMem for task completion. The system treats focused contributions consisting of an explicit hypothesis paired with a validation attempt as valid outputs, does not treat negative results as failed projects by default, and retains all successful, weak, and failed trajectories for audit rather than curating a selected set of successful demonstrations. The current version is scoped to AI/ML research, where most hypotheses can be operationalized as executable code evaluated on standard shared benchmarks.","experiments":"In its first continuous public deployment, FARS produced 166 complete research papers covering 67 fine-grained AI/ML subtopics. This leads to degraded reasoning performance within fixed context window constraints.","importance":"This work provides a practical, training-free plugin for agent long-context management. It offers a useful design insight for long-context pretraining research: context does not need to be a static linear concatenation of history. Instead, high-information-density visible context can be dynamically assembled from an underlying lossless store to match current inference needs, reducing information density decay and attention dispersion over long sequences.","technical_approach":"ACE uses a decoupled two-layer design. The first layer is a lossless message maintenance store that permanently retains raw messages and precomputed compressed abstractions for every historical step, with no overwrites. Before each agent decision step, a separate context orchestration layer is rebuilt from scratch. It assigns each historical message one of three fully reversible states based on current task state: keep raw content, use compressed abstraction, or temporarily exclude. State assignments only affect the context passed to the reasoning model at the current step, with no changes to underlying storage. Temporarily excluded messages can be reintroduced, and abstracted messages can be expanded to raw text in any future step. The module runs as an external wrapper around the existing agent reasoning loop, requiring no changes to agent action spaces, message flows, or model training.","experiments":"On the standard ReAct framework, ACE outperforms truncation and compression baselines on long-horizon agent tasks.
